D[i][k] is local to\n// the rank, so no other communication is required.\n__global__ void fwIterationKernel(unsigned int* __restrict__ dist,\n                                  unsigned int* __restrict__ path,\n                                  const unsigned int* __restrict__ rowk,\n                                  const int n, const int localN,\n                                  const int k) {\n    const int j = blockIdx.x * blockDim.x + threadIdx.x;\n    const int li = blockIdx.y * blockDim.y + threadIdx.y;\n    if (j >= n || li >= localN) {\n        return;\n    }\n\n    const size_t rowOffset = static_cast<size_t>(li) * static_cast<size_t>(n);\n    const unsigned int distIK = dist[rowOffset + static_cast<size_t>(k)];\n    const unsigned int newDist = distIK + rowk[j];\n    const size_t idx = rowOffset + static_cast<size_t>(j);\n    if (newDist < dist[idx]) {\n        dist[idx] = newDist;\n        path[idx] = static_cast<unsigned int>(k);\n    }\n}\n\nvoid initializeDistanceMatrix(std::vector<unsigned int>& dist, const size_t numNodes,\n                              const unsigned int rangeMin, const unsigned int rangeMax) {\n    unsigned int seed = 42;\n    const double range = static_cast<double>(rangeMax - rangeMin) + 1.0;\n\n    for (size_t i = 0; i < numNodes * numNodes; ++i) {\n        dist[i] = rangeMin + (unsigned int)(range * rand_r(&seed) / (double)RAND_MAX);\n    }\n\n    // Set diagonal to zero (distance from node to itself is 0)\n    for (size_t i = 0; i < numNodes; ++i) {\n        dist[idx2(i, i, numNodes)] = 0;\n    }\n}\n\nbool validateResult(const std::vector<unsigned int>& dist, const size_t numNodes) {\n    // Basic sanity checks\n\n    // 1. j < std::min(numNodes, static_cast<size_t>(10)); ++j) {\n            for (size_t k = 0; k < numNodes; ++k) {\n                const unsigned int distIJ = dist[idx2(j, i, numNodes)];\n                const unsigned int distIK = dist[idx2(k, i, numNodes)];\n                const unsigned int distKJ = dist[idx2(j, k, numNodes)];\n\n                // Check for overflow before addition\n                if (distIK < INF && distKJ < INF) {\n                    if (distIK + distKJ < distIJ) {\n                        printf(\"Validation failed: triangle inequality violated at [%zu,%zu,%zu]\\n\",\n                               i, j, k);\n                        return false;\n                    }\n                }\n            }\n        }\n    }\n\n    return true;\n}\n\nvoid printUsage(const char* progName) {\n    printf(\"Usage: %s [options]\\n\", progName);\n    printf(\"Options:\\n\");\n    printf(\"  -n <num>     Number of nodes in the graph (default: 512)\\n\");\n    printf(\"  -v           Enable validation\\n\");\n    printf(\"  -r           Print results for external validation\\n\");\n    printf(\"  -h           Show this help message\\n\");\n}\n\nint ownerOf(const std::vector<int>& starts, const int k) {\n    // starts[r] is the first row owned by rank r.\n    const auto it = std::upper_bound(starts.begin(), starts.end(), k);\n    return static_cast<int>(it - starts.begin()) - 1;\n}\n\nint main(int argc, char** argv) {\n    int provided = MPI_THREAD_SINGLE;\n    MPI_Init_thread(&argc, &argv, MPI_THREAD_FUNNELED, &provided);\n\n    int rank = 0;\n    int nranks = 1;\n    MPI_Comm_rank(MPI_COMM_WORLD, &rank);\n    MPI_Comm_size(MPI_COMM_WORLD, &nranks);\n\n    size_t numNodes = 512;\n    bool validate = false;\n    bool printResults = false;\n    bool showHelp = false;\n    bool badOption = false;\n\n    // Parse command line arguments\n    for (int i = 1; {"type":"reasoning","timestamp":1792603413119,"sessionID":"ses_ea08dd3eeffe5ahS79FvV2eZ03","part":{"id":"prt_15f7d4628001ZL8oOFm4vmsiNf","messageID":"msg_15f7d401d001bswDdtTXOagW6Z","sessionID":"ses_ea08dd3eeffe5ahS79FvV2eZ03","type":"reasoning","text":"Now let's think whether we can improve small-n performance by removing the unconditional sync on non-owner? We fixed race by syncing. Could instead use double-buffered hRow and dRow to avoid full sync? The owner's D2H still requires waiting for its kernel k-1. All ranks need kernel k-1 complete before applying kernel k (same stream ordering handles). Non-owner could receive row k into hRow[parity] while kernel k-1 still runs, but the broadcast won't complete until owner finishes kernel k-1 anyway. So sync cost is negligible. Good.\n\nBut could we overlap Bcast with previous kernel on non-owner, then sync before H2D? That would hide communication behind compute. Currently we sync then Bcast; non-owner idle during Bcast while its GPU could be finishing kernel k-1? Wait dependency: to compute kernel k it needs row k. But non-owner kernel k-1 must finish anyway before kernel k due to stream. If we issue Bcast while kernel k-1 runs, we could overlap. But hRow buffer race with previous H2D from k-1? By the time kernel k-1 is running, its H2D (of row k-1) has completed (same stream order: H2D then kernel). So hRow is free once kernel k-1 has *started*? Actually H2D completed before kernel k-1 starts, so hRow safe to overwrite while kernel k-1 runs. dRow is being read by kernel k-1, so we need a separate dRow buffer for k (double buffering) to upload row k before kernel k-1 finishes. Then issue kernel k which waits in stream for kernel k-1. This would overlap Bcast with compute. But owner cannot send row k until its kernel k-1 finishes (row k updated by k-1). So owner still syncs; non-owners could receive while computing. Since owner is the bottleneck (sync before send), overlap on non-owner helps hide their compute. Could improve np>1. But there is the \"blocked\" Floyd-Warshall where you process a block of K pivot nodes: phase 1 computes within pivot block sequentially (on one rank/GPU) then broadcast pivot block; phase 2 updates block rows/cols; phase 3 updates rest. Blocked FW reduces collectives to n/B, each O(B*n) data; same total bytes, fewer latencies. For n=512 with B=64, 8 rounds instead of 512, huge latency reduction.
